</tr><tr><td class="sidebar-navbar" style="padding-top:0;"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1129693374"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1063604349">.mw-parser-output .navbar{display:inline;font-size:88%;font-weight:normal}.mw-parser-output .navbar-collapse{float:left;text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .navbar-boxtext{word-spacing:0}.mw-parser-output .navbar ul{display:inline-block;white-space:nowrap;line-height:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::before{margin-right:-0.125em;content:"[ "}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::after{margin-left:-0.125em;content:" ]"}.mw-parser-output .navbar li{word-spacing:-0.125em}.mw-parser-output .navbar a>span,.mw-parser-output .navbar a>abbr{text-decoration:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-mini abbr{font-variant:small-caps;border-bottom:none;text-decoration:none;cursor:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-ct-full{font-size:114%;margin:0 7em}.mw-parser-output .navbar-ct-mini{font-size:114%;margin:0 4em}</style><div class="navbar plainlinks hlist navbar-mini"><ul><li class="nv-view"><a href="/wiki/Template:Saint_Thomas_Christians" title="Template:Saint Thomas Christians"><abbr title="View this template">v</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-talk"><a href="/wiki/Template_talk:Saint_Thomas_Christians" title="Template talk:Saint Thomas Christians"><abbr title="Discuss this template">t</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-edit"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Template:Saint_Thomas_Christians&amp;action=edit"><abbr title="Edit this template">e</abbr></a></li></ul></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Kuriakose Elias Chavara</b>, <a href="/wiki/Carmelites_of_Mary_Immaculate" title="Carmelites of Mary Immaculate">CMI</a> (10 February 1805 – 3 January 1871) was an Indian <a href="/wiki/Syro-Malabar_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Syro-Malabar Church">Syro-Malabar Catholic</a> priest, religious, <a href="/wiki/Philosopher" class="mw-redirect" title="Philosopher">philosopher</a> and <a href="/wiki/Social_reformer" class="mw-redirect" title="Social reformer">social reformer</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-roshnikma.blogspot.de_2-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-roshnikma.blogspot.de-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> He is the first canonised Catholic male saint of Indian origin and a member of the <a href="/wiki/Syro-Malabar_Catholic_Church" title="Syro-Malabar Catholic Church">Syro-Malabar Church</a>, an <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Catholic_Churches" title="Eastern Catholic Churches">Eastern Catholic church</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-cnnibn_3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cnnibn-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-malayalamanorama2_4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-malayalamanorama2-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> He was the co-founder and first <a href="/wiki/Prior_(ecclesiastical)" title="Prior (ecclesiastical)">Prior General</a> of the first <a href="/wiki/Religious_congregation" title="Religious congregation">congregation</a> for men in the Syro-Malabar Church, now known as the <a href="/wiki/Carmelites_of_Mary_Immaculate" title="Carmelites of Mary Immaculate">Carmelites of Mary Immaculate</a> (CMI), and of a similar one for women, the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_of_the_Mother_of_Carmel" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation of the Mother of Carmel">Congregation of the Mother of Carmel</a> (CMC). He was a pioneer in many fields.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life">Early life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Kuriakose Elias Chavara was born on 10 February 1805 at <a href="/wiki/Kainakary" title="Kainakary">Kainakary</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kerala" title="Kerala">Kerala</a> in a Nasrani Christian family as the son of Iko (Kuriakose) Chavara and Mariam Thoppil. Nasranis are <a href="/wiki/Saint_Thomas_Christians" title="Saint Thomas Christians">Saint Thomas Christians</a> (also known as Syriac Christians) who trace their lineage to the ancient Christians of Kerala baptised by <a href="/wiki/Thomas_the_Apostle" title="Thomas the Apostle">Thomas the Apostle</a>. The name <i>Kuriakose</i> is derived from the <a href="/wiki/Syriac_language" title="Syriac language">Syriac Aramaic</a> name ܩܘܪܝܩܘܣ (<a href="/wiki/Quriaqos_and_Julietta" class="mw-redirect" title="Quriaqos and Julietta"><i>Quriaqos</i></a>).<sup id="cite_ref-malayalamanorama_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-malayalamanorama-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> He was baptised on 17 February 1805 at <a href="/wiki/St._Joseph%27s_Syro-Malabar_Catholic_Church,_Chennamkary" class="mw-redirect" title="St. Joseph&#39;s Syro-Malabar Catholic Church, Chennamkary">St. Joseph's Syro-Malabar Catholic Church, Chennamkary</a>. On 8 September 1805, Chavara was dedicated to Blessed Virgin Mary at <a href="/wiki/St._Mary%27s_Church,_Vechoor" title="St. Mary&#39;s Church, Vechoor">St. Mary's Church, Vechoor</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> The Chavara family has derived from the ancient Nasrani family Meenappally in Kainakary.
</p><p>In his childhood, Kuriakose attended the village school. There he studied language and elementary sciences. He entered the seminary in 1818 in <a href="/wiki/Pallipuram,_Alappuzha" class="mw-redirect" title="Pallipuram, Alappuzha">Pallipuram</a> where <a href="/wiki/Palackal_Thoma_Malpan" class="mw-redirect" title="Palackal Thoma Malpan">Palackal Thoma Malpan</a> was the <a href="/wiki/Rector_(ecclesiastical)" title="Rector (ecclesiastical)">Rector</a>. He was <a href="/wiki/Holy_Orders" class="mw-redirect" title="Holy Orders">ordained</a> a priest on 29 November 1829 and celebrated first <a href="/wiki/Holy_Qurbana" title="Holy Qurbana">Holy Qurbana</a> at St. Andrew's <a href="/wiki/Catholic" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholic">Catholic</a> Forane Church Arthunkal Alappuzha. His special intention during the first Holy Qurabana was the realization of the religious institute which was being contemplated by <a href="/wiki/Palackal_Thoma" title="Palackal Thoma">Palackal Thomas</a> Malpan, Porukara Thomas
Kathanar, Brother Jacob Kaniathara and himself.<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Later_life">Later life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Later life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Kuriakose Elias Chavara joined with two other priests, <a href="/wiki/Palackal_Thoma_Malpan" class="mw-redirect" title="Palackal Thoma Malpan">Palackal Thoma Malpan</a> and Porukara Thoma Kathanar to lead a monastic life. The name of the community they founded was <i>Servants of Mary Immaculate</i>. The foundation for the first monastery at Mannanam was laid on 11 May 1831 by Porukara Thomas Kathanar. Palackal Malpan and Porukara Kathanar died in 1841 and 1846 respectively. On 8 December 1855, Kuriakose Elias Chavara and ten other priests took vows in the Carmelite tradition. He was nominated as the Prior General of Mannanam monastery. The congregation became affiliated as a <a href="/wiki/Third_Order" class="mw-redirect" title="Third Order">Third Order</a> institute of the <a href="/wiki/Discalced_Carmelites" title="Discalced Carmelites">Discalced Carmelites</a>. From that point on they used the <a href="/wiki/Postnominal" class="mw-redirect" title="Postnominal">postnominal</a> initials of TOCD.<sup id="cite_ref-CMI_2014_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CMI_2014-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Social_reformer">Social reformer</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Social reformer">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Kuriakose Elias Chavara initiated reforms in the <a href="/wiki/Kerala" title="Kerala">Kerala</a> society much before <a href="/wiki/Narayana_Guru" title="Narayana Guru">Narayana Guru</a>(1853) <a href="/wiki/Chattampi_Swamikal" title="Chattampi Swamikal">Chattambi Swamikal</a>(1853) and <a href="/wiki/Vakkom_Moulavi" title="Vakkom Moulavi">Vakkom Abdul Khadar Maulavi</a>(1854).<sup id="cite_ref-indianexpress_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-indianexpress-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Mathew_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mathew-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> Though he hailed from a <a href="/wiki/Saint_Thomas_Christians" title="Saint Thomas Christians">Syriac Christian</a> family,<sup id="cite_ref-Forrester_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Forrester-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> which occupied a higher social status, he played a major role in educating and uplifting people especially of the lower ranks of society.<sup id="cite_ref-roshnikma.blogspot.de_2-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-roshnikma.blogspot.de-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Kuriakose Chavara started an institution for Sanskrit studies at Mannanam in 1846.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (March 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> A tutor belonging to the <a href="/wiki/Variar" title="Variar">Variar</a> community was brought from Thrissur, to teach at this Sanskrit institution. After establishing the Sanskrit institution in Mannanam, Chavara took the initiative to start a school in a nearby village called Arpookara. On this Parappurath Varkey wrote in the Chronicles of the Mannanam monastery: “While the work on the Mannanam School began, a place on the Arpookara Thuruthumali hill was located to build a Chapel and school for the converts from the <a href="/wiki/Pulayar" title="Pulayar">Pulaya</a> caste."<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> Chavara was the first Indian who not only dared to admit the untouchables to schools but also provided them with Sanskrit education which was forbidden to the lower castes, thereby challenging social bans based on caste, as early as the former part of the 19th century.<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>It was during this time Bishop Bernadine Baccinelly issued a circular in 1856 which would act as the root cause of tremendous growth of education and hundred percent literacy in Kerala. Kuriakose Chavara was the motivator for such a movement and he successfully convinced Bishop Bernadine to issue a circular, apparently as an order. It was a warning circular which stated, “each parish should establish educational institutions, or else they will be debarred from the communion”.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (March 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> The schools in Kerala are commonly called <i><a href="/wiki/Pallikoodam" title="Pallikoodam">Pallikudams</a></i> (school attached to Church (Palli)) because of this circular.<sup id="cite_ref-malayalamanorama_5-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-malayalamanorama-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Kureethara_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kureethara-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> Kuriakose Chavara took great interest in implementing the circular. He delegated the members of his Congregation to ensure the implementation of the order in the circular and to actively take up educational activities. Each monastery was to oversee these activities of the parish churches in its neighbourhood.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Kureethara_14-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kureethara-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Midday_meal">Midday meal</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Midday meal">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Kuriakose Chavara knew that the schools he started in Mannanam and Arpookara would be successful if the poor students especially dalits were given <a href="/wiki/Midday_Meal_Scheme" title="Midday Meal Scheme">midday meals</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-roshnikma.blogspot.de_2-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-roshnikma.blogspot.de-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> It was his original idea. It inspired <a href="/wiki/C._P._Ramaswami_Iyer" title="C. P. Ramaswami Iyer">Sir C P Ramaswamy Iyer</a> to recommend this to King for being implemented in all government run schools.<sup id="cite_ref-Mathew_10-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mathew-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> This practice is continued even today in queens government schools in India.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Pidiyari">Pidiyari</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Pidiyari">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Kuriakose Chavara started a charity practice known as <i>Pidiyari</i> (a handful of rice) to encourage people to make daily small donations to help the needy.<sup id="cite_ref-Pidyari_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pidyari-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> The <i>Pidyari</i> scheme supported the Midday meal Kuriakose Chavara popularized in schools<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> The <i>Pidiyari</i> scheme was implemented in the following way: Participants would daily set aside a small quantity of rice in a special collection pot. The rice collected would be brought to Church during the weekends and was used to feed the poor, especially students for midday meal.<sup id="cite_ref-Pidyari_16-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pidyari-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>  A pious organization was formed by Kuriakose Chavara called “Unnimishihayude Dharma Sabha” who took care of the <i>Pidiyari</i>collection.<sup id="cite_ref-roshnikma.blogspot.de_2-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-roshnikma.blogspot.de-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Printing_press">Printing press</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Printing press">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Kuriakose Chavara started St. Joseph's Press at Mannanam<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> in 1846, which was the third printing press in Kerala and the first press founded by a <a href="/wiki/Malayali" title="Malayali">Malayali</a> without the help of foreigners.<sup id="cite_ref-indianexpress_9-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-indianexpress-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> From this printing press came the oldest existing Malayalam newspaper in circulation <i><a href="/wiki/Deepika_(newspaper)" title="Deepika (newspaper)">Nasrani Deepika</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-indianexpress_9-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-indianexpress-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Deepika_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Deepika-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Service_to_the_Church">Service to the Church</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Service to the Church">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Kuriakose Elias Chavara introduced retreat preaching for the laity for the first time in the Kerala Church. He popularised devotions and piety exercises such as rosary, way of the cross and eucharistic adoration. He was the <a href="/wiki/Vicar_General" class="mw-redirect" title="Vicar General">Vicar General</a> of <a href="/wiki/Syro-Malabar" class="mw-redirect" title="Syro-Malabar">Syriac Rite Catholics</a><sup id="cite_ref-indianexpress_9-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-indianexpress-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-manorama_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-manorama-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> in 1861 in order to counter the influence of Mar Thomas Rochos on <a href="/wiki/Saint_Thomas_Christians" title="Saint Thomas Christians">Saint Thomas Christians</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Congregations_founded">Congregations founded</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Congregations founded">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="CMI_Congregation">CMI Congregation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: CMI Congregation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In co-operation with Palackal Thoma Malpan and Thoma Porukara, Kuriakose Elias Chavara founded an Indian religious congregation for men, now known as the <a href="/wiki/Carmelites_of_Mary_Immaculate" title="Carmelites of Mary Immaculate">Carmelites of Mary Immaculate</a>. Chavara took religious vows on 8 December 1855 and took the name of Kuriakose Elias of the Holy Family.<sup id="cite_ref-cmi.in_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cmi.in-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Kuriakose Elias Chavara was the Prior General of all the monasteries of the congregation from 1856 till his death in 1871.He was commonly called under the name 'Common Prior'.<sup id="cite_ref-cmi.in_23-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cmi.in-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> The activities of the members of CMI congregation under the leadership of Chavara created huge transformation in the society. This made priests and people to request Chavara to open religious houses in their area. He established seven new monasteries besides Mannanam. They are Koonammavu-1857, <a href="/wiki/Elthuruth" title="Elthuruth">Elthuruth</a>-(<a href="/wiki/St._Aloysius_College,_Thrissur" title="St. Aloysius College, Thrissur">St. Aloysius College, Thrissur</a>)1858, Plasnal-1858, Vazhakulam-1859, Pulincunnu-1861, Ambazhakad-1868, and Mutholy-1870. In 1864, The Vicar Apostolic transferred St.Chavara to Koonammavu Monastery.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-cmi.in_23-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cmi.in-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Carmelite_Congregation_for_Women">Carmelite Congregation for Women</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Carmelite Congregation for Women">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1866, 13 February, Kuriakose Elias Chavara founded the first Carmelite convent for women at Koonamavu under the name 'Third Order of Carmelites Discalced' which would later become <a href="/wiki/Congregation_of_Mother_of_Carmel" title="Congregation of Mother of Carmel">CMC</a> and <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://ctcsisters.com/">CTC Congregation</a> in Syro Malabar Church and Latin Church respectively.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> While CMC congregation acknowledges and upholds the role of Kuriakose Chavara in their foundation, CTC congregation denies any role for him and considers Mother Eliswa as the foundress.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Kuriakose Chavara hoped and prayed for the establishment of a religious congregation for women in the apostolic Church of St. Thomas. According to Kuriakose Chavara the lack of convents was a 'pathetic situation,' which led to deep sorrow within him.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> He conceived the convent as a house of sanctity where the girls could learn spiritual matters, grow up as good Christians and work for the intellectual development and education of women to achieve social welfare.<sup id="cite_ref-CMC_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CMC-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Leopold Beccaro – who was a close associate and confessor of Kuriakose Chavara – with whom Eliswa had communicated her desire to lead a life of chastity, during her meetings with him for confession and spiritual direction, wrote in Italian in his personal diary on 3 January 1871, the day of the death of Chavara: “The founder and the first prior of the Tertiaries of the Discalced Carmelites in Malabar, who with extreme fatigue has founded the monastery of the sisters [e fondato con somme fatiche il monastero delle Monache]...”<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> Again, in another important document, a short biography of Chavara written by Beccaro himself, we come across the following affirmative statements: “Among these, specially, [he] earnestly desired to bring into existence an abode of virtues for the girls of Malayalam and a convent of sisters for learning doctrines and traditions of the Catholic religion as well as to make them grow as good Christian children...<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> It is a fact known to all that even after the starting of the convent, he showed great fervour and interest to conduct everything in order and with virtues...” These two statements made by Beccaro give credence to the fact that Chavara had not only a deep and long-lasting desire to establish a convent for sisters, but had also made every effort, including the spiritual and administrative guidance in the realization of the project.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Writings">Writings</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Writings">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>All the literary works of Kuriakose Chavara were written between 1829 and 1870. The literary writings of Kuriakose Chavara are unique in two aspects. First, it reflects the religious spiritualism of Christianity. Second, even after a century after the Kuriakose Chavara wrote, there are limited number of literary works with reference to Christianity.<sup id="cite_ref-Elizabeth_Molly_2004_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Elizabeth_Molly_2004-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The writings of Kuriakose Elias Chavara can be divided generally into following categories during the namely:<sup id="cite_ref-saintchavara.blogspot.de_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-saintchavara.blogspot.de-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Elizabeth_Molly_2004_32-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Elizabeth_Molly_2004-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li>Chronics and historical writings</li>
<li>Spiritual writings</li>
<li>Letters</li>
<li>Prayer Texts</li>
<li>Writings on Liturgy</li>
<li>Writings related to administration</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Chronicles_and_historical_writings">Chronicles and historical writings</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Chronicles and historical writings">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The Chronicles (Nalagamangal) narrates the daily events not only of the monastery but also of the society. These writings were originally found in Malayalam, in Kuriakose Chavara's own handwriting.<sup id="cite_ref-Elizabeth_Molly_2004_32-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Elizabeth_Molly_2004-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> There are two manuscripts under the title 'Mannanam Nalagamangal.' Together with the events of the CMI Congregation the chronicles depict events associated with the Society and Kerala Church of that time. The book 'Complete Works of Chavara', Vol. 1 published in 1990 contains five Chronicles.<sup id="cite_ref-saintchavara.blogspot.de_33-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-saintchavara.blogspot.de-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Spiritual_writings">Spiritual writings</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Spiritual writings">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<h4><span id="Atmanuthapam_.28The_lamentations_of_a_Repentant_Soul.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Atmanuthapam_(The_lamentations_of_a_Repentant_Soul)">Atmanuthapam (The lamentations of a Repentant Soul)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Atmanuthapam (The lamentations of a Repentant Soul)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>It is an autobiographical poem written in epic style – Mahakavyam (<a href="/wiki/Mahakavya" title="Mahakavya">Mahakavya</a>). The poem gives life to life of Jesus from birth to ascension into heaven and life of Mary from ascension of Jesus to assumption of Mary into heaven. The uniqueness of the poem is that Kuriakose Chavara brings his own life experiences from childhood.
</p><p>According to Dr. <a href="/wiki/K._M._Tharakan" title="K. M. Tharakan">K.M.Tharakan</a>  Atmanuthapam reflects Kuriakose Chavara's philosophy of life which in turn exemplifies a Christian religious life. Dr. Tharakan states, "...Humility, which is not self-contempt, and repentance are the foundation stones of the philosophy of his life. Thus the first step is faith in God. The second is the belief that one can reach God through Jesus Christ. The third is the conviction that without repenting and accepting Jesus, one cannot reach Christ... what a Christian obtains is the awareness of the noble qualities. These qualities are 'faith', 'hope' and 'love'. Only through these triple qualities, a Christian can attain Christ."
<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span id="Maranaveetil_Cholvanulla_Parvam_.28A_poem_to_be_sung_in_the_bereaved_house.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Maranaveetil_Cholvanulla_Parvam_(A_poem_to_be_sung_in_the_bereaved_house)">Maranaveetil Cholvanulla Parvam (A poem to be sung in the bereaved house)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Maranaveetil Cholvanulla Parvam (A poem to be sung in the bereaved house)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>It is intended to be sung when the corpus is placed in the coffin for public homage.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> The poem contains 1162 verses. The underlying message is that one should lead a virtuous and God-fearing life in order overcome the painful experience of death. The articles of faith related to the death of a man are enumerated and beautifully illustrated with stories or incidents, the poet heard or read.<sup id="cite_ref-Elizabeth_Molly_2004_32-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Elizabeth_Molly_2004-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span id="Nalla_Appante_Chavarul_.28Testament_of_a_Loving_Father.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Nalla_Appante_Chavarul_(Testament_of_a_Loving_Father)">Nalla Appante Chavarul (Testament of a Loving Father)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Nalla Appante Chavarul (Testament of a Loving Father)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>It is the counsel to the Christian Families of Kainakari parish but its relevance to the families resonates till date. This insightful and down-to-earth document is the first of its kind in the known history of the Church, offers practical direction to families in leading a God-fearing as well as socially commendable life.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span id="Anastasiayude_Rakthasakshyam_.28The_martyrdom_of_Anastacia.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Anastasiayude_Rakthasakshyam_(The_martyrdom_of_Anastacia)">Anastasiayude Rakthasakshyam (The martyrdom of Anastacia)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Anastasiayude Rakthasakshyam (The martyrdom of Anastacia)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>It contains 232 verses.<sup id="cite_ref-Elizabeth_Molly_2004_32-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Elizabeth_Molly_2004-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span id="Dhyana_Sallapangal_.28Colloquies_in_meditation.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Dhyana_Sallapangal_(Colloquies_in_meditation)">Dhyana Sallapangal (Colloquies in meditation)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Dhyana Sallapangal (Colloquies in meditation)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p><i>Dhyana Sallapangal</i> consists of the reflective meditative notes of Kuriakose Chavara.<sup id="cite_ref-Elizabeth_Molly_2004_32-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Elizabeth_Molly_2004-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span id="Eclogues_.28Shepherd_Plays.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Eclogues_(Shepherd_Plays)">Eclogues (Shepherd Plays)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Eclogues (Shepherd Plays)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>They were the first dramatic plays in Malayalam in the pastoral (shepherd) genre or <a href="/wiki/Eclogue" title="Eclogue">Eclogues</a> of <a href="/wiki/Italy" title="Italy">Italy</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> They were plays written for being performed during the Christmas season. Kuriakose Chavara wrote it between 1856 – 1858. He wrote 10 Eclogues (Shepherd Plays) and introduced them in the novitiate at Koonammavu Seminary.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The first Indian drama is Neeldarpan, published in 1860 and is written in Bengali by <a href="/wiki/Dinabandhu_Mitra" title="Dinabandhu Mitra">Dinabandhu Mitra</a> on the miseries of the indigo cultivators. The first Malayalam play is considered Abhinjana Shakuntalam, translated into Malayalam by Kerala Verma Valiakovil Thampuran in 1882. Kuriakose Chavara wrote the 10 eclogues or liturgical dramas decades before. He could be considered the father of Malayalam drama.<sup id="cite_ref-saintchavara.blogspot.de_33-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-saintchavara.blogspot.de-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Letters">Letters</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Letters">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>These letters were written on different occasions to various persons or groups. There are 67 original copies of these letters. These letters are published in 'Complete Works of Chavara' Vol.IV. The chronicle in Koonammavu records three letters written to them by Kuriakose Elias Chavara. Even though the Chronicle states many letters and notes were written by Chavara, only three are recorded.<sup id="cite_ref-dhruwadeepti.blogspot.de_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-dhruwadeepti.blogspot.de-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Prayer_texts">Prayer texts</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Prayer texts">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Even though Kuriakose Chavara wrote many prayer texts only six of them have been preserved. They are: 
i) Meditation: method of beginning – Meditation: method of beginning is an intercessory prayer to mother Mary seeking help to be with him during the meditation.
ii) Prayer to the Blessed virgin Mary. Thanksgiving prayer to the blessings that received from mother Mary.
iii) Morning Prayer to Most Holy Trinity – Thanksgiving prayer for the favours received.
iv) Evening prayer to Most Holy Trinity  – Thanksgiving prayer for the favours received. 
v) Prayer for custody of eyes – Prayers to be recited by priests to remain rooted in their fidelity to Jesus Christ. 
vi) Prayer to be recited by priests – Prayers to be recited by priests to remain rooted in their fidelity to Jesus Christ.<sup id="cite_ref-Elizabeth_Molly_2004_32-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Elizabeth_Molly_2004-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Writings_on_Liturgy">Writings on Liturgy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Writings on Liturgy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Kuriakose Chavara wrote a number of liturgical texts that played an important role in reforming liturgy.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> They include the Divine office for priests, Divine office for the dead, office of the Blessed virgin Mary, prayers of various blessings, the order of Holy mass – <i>Tukasa</i>, liturgical calendar, forty hours adoration and prayer books for lay man.<sup id="cite_ref-Elizabeth_Molly_2004_32-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Elizabeth_Molly_2004-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Writings_related_to_administration">Writings related to administration</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Writings related to administration">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In this category there are 16 writings that has been preserved. Some of these are written on palm leaves and others on paper. It deals with matters pertaining to administrative matters pertaining to the time of Chavara.<sup id="cite_ref-saintchavara.blogspot.de_33-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-saintchavara.blogspot.de-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Prior_Mango">Prior Mango</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Prior Mango">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Prior Mango (പ്രിയോർ മാങ്ങ)  is a variety of mango which Kuriakose Chavara popularised. It is named after him who was endearingly called “Prior” based on his position as the Prior or head of the religious congregation.<sup id="cite_ref-saintchavara.blogspot.de_33-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-saintchavara.blogspot.de-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> At Mannanam monastery there was a mango tree which was planted and taken care of by Kuriakose Chavara. He sent the mango seedlings to monasteries and convents and told the members: Please plant the sapling of this sweet mango, which I name it as ‘Dukran’(Orma = memory) in each of our monastery. "This is to make you realize that myself and all men are weak and faltering and don’t have long life even as these mango trees which give sweet fruits."<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup>  In a letter he wrote&#160;: " This Mango-tree (1870) and its seedlings leave a loving patrimonial memory for us"<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> It is because of the association of Carmelite Prior Kuriakose Chavara that this variety of mango came to be known all over Kerala as "Priormavu" (the mango tree of the Prior). Prior Mango is considered as one of the premium variety of mangoes in Kerala and is often exported to its neighbouring south Indian states.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
He also planted prior mango tree in koonammav at St: philominas church backyard. The tree still stands and bears fruit throughout the year.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Death">Death</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Death">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Kuriakose Elias Chavara died on 3 January 1871, aged 66, at Koonammavu. He was buried in <a href="/wiki/St.Philomena%27s_Forane_Church,_Koonammavu" class="mw-redirect" title="St.Philomena&#39;s Forane Church, Koonammavu">St.Philomena's Forane Church, Koonammavu</a><sup id="cite_ref-indianexpress_9-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-indianexpress-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-manorama_21-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-manorama-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> His body was later moved to <a href="/wiki/St._Joseph%27s_Church,_Mannanam" class="mw-redirect" title="St. Joseph&#39;s Church, Mannanam">St. Joseph's Monastery Church</a> in Mannanam.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> His memorial is celebrated on 3 January as per the Syro-Malabar liturgical calendar.<sup id="cite_ref-catholicorg_47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-catholicorg-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> whereas his memorial is celebrated on 18 February as per the Roman Liturgical Calendar of the Latin Rite.
</p><p>The following were the last words of Kuriakose Chavara: “Why are you sad? All God’s people must die some day. My hour has come. By the grace of God, I prepared myself for it since long.” Showing a picture of the Holy Family, he continued, "My parents taught me to keep the Holy Family always in my mind and to honour them throughout my life. As I had always the protection of the Holy Family I can tell you with confidence that I have never lost the baptismal grace I received in baptism. I dedicate our little Congregation and each of you to the Holy Family. Always rely on Jesus, Mary and Joseph. Let the Holy Family reign in your hearts. Don’t be sad about my dying. Joyfully submit yourselves to the will of God. God is all powerful and His blessings are countless. God will provide you with a new Prior who will be a source of blessing for the Congregation as well as for you. Hold fast to the constitution, the rules of our elders and that of the Church. Love our Lord Jesus in the Blessed Sacrament with all your heart. Draw the waters of eternal life from that fountain as in the words of the Prophet Elijah. All the members of the congregation, especially elders must be charitable to one another. If you do so, God will be glorified by the congregation and which will be flourished day after day. Your charity will bring salvation to souls."<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Miracles">Miracles</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Miracles">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Scores of miraculous favours were reported by the intercession of Kuriakose Chavara. <a href="/wiki/Alphonsa_of_the_Immaculate_Conception" title="Alphonsa of the Immaculate Conception">Alphonsa of the Immaculate Conception</a>, who later became the first saint of India, has testified in 1936 that Kuriakose Elias Chavara had appeared to her twice during her illness and relieved her suffering. Alphonsa had a holy relic of Chavra's hair which was taken by one of his disciples Varkey Muttathupadathu and which she believed allowed her to pray to Kuriakose Chavara and receive miraculous cure. The relic is now preserved in Mannanam.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Beatification">Beatification</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Beatification">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The miracle which Rome approved for the <a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">beatification</a> of Kuriakose Chavara was the cure of the congenial deformity of the legs (<a href="/wiki/Clubfoot" title="Clubfoot">clubfoot</a>) of Joseph Mathew Pennaparambil happened in April 1960.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> Joseph was born club-footed with congenial deformity of both the legs. On hearing that many miracles have happened through the intercession of Kuriakose Chavara, Joseph and his family started praying. They prayed almost a month. One day when Joseph and his sister were walking back from school, she asked him to pray to Saint Kuriakose Elias Chavara for the cure of his legs and asked him to recite 1 Our Father, 1 Hail Mary and 1 Glory be to the Father. As they walked reciting prayers suddenly Joseph's leg started shivering. Joseph pressed his right leg to the ground and he could now walk properly with right leg. They continued their prayers and on 30 April 1960, while Joseph and his sister was on the way to elder brother's house, the left leg too became normal. Since then he could walk normally. Joseph believes that it was the intercession Kuriakose Chavara which resulted in the miracle. Rome approved the miracle which led to the beatification of Kuriakose Chavara as Blessed in 1986.<sup id="cite_ref-chavarahead_51-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-chavarahead-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Canonization">Canonization</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Canonization">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The miracle which was approved for <a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">canonization</a> of Kuriakose Chavara to sainthood was the instantaneous, total and stable cure of the congenital squint (alternating esotropia) in both eyes of Maria Jose Kottarathil, a Catholic girl of age 9 from Pala in <a href="/wiki/Kottayam" title="Kottayam">Kottayam</a> District of Kerala State in India.<sup id="cite_ref-chavarahead_51-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-chavarahead-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> Even though Maria was suggested to have surgery by five doctors, Maria and her family decided to pray to Kuriakose Chavara. On 12 October 2007, Maria visited the room and tomb of Kuriakose Chavara at Mannanam with her parents. On 16 October 2007, the squint eyes disappeared. The miracle was approved by the Congregation for the Causes of the Saints on 18 March 2014 which lead to the canonization.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Chronicle_of_Canonization">Chronicle of Canonization</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Chronicle of Canonization">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The official canonization process of Kuriakose Chavara started in 1955, Mar Mathew Kavukattu, arch-bishop of Changanacherry, received instructions from Rome to start diocese-level procedure towards the canonisation. On 7 April 1984, Pope John Paul II approved Kuriakose Elias Chavara's practice of heroic virtues and declared him <i>Venerable</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-BlessedCh_53-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BlessedCh-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> Kuriakose Elias Chavara was <a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">beatified</a> at Kottayam on 8 February 1986 by <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a> in the course of a papal visit to India.<sup id="cite_ref-BlessedCh_53-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BlessedCh-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 3 April 2014, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Francis" title="Pope Francis">Pope Francis</a> authorised the Congregation for the Causes of Saints to promulgate the decrees concerning the miracle attributed to Kuriakose Kathanar's intercession.<sup id="cite_ref-VIS_2014_54-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-VIS_2014-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> This confirmed Pope's approval of Kuriakose Elias Chavara's canonisation.<sup id="cite_ref-catholicregister_2014_55-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-catholicregister_2014-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> On 23 November 2014, he was canonised at <a href="/wiki/Saint_Peter%27s_Square" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Peter&#39;s Square">Saint Peter's Square</a> by Pope Francis along with <a href="/wiki/Euphrasia_Eluvathingal" title="Euphrasia Eluvathingal">Euphrasia Eluvathingal</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Radio_Vaticana_56-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Radio_Vaticana-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> Pope Francis stated that "Father Kuriakose Elias was a religious, both active and contemplative, who generously gave his life for the Syro-Malabar Church, putting into action the maxim “sanctification of oneself and the salvation of others."<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Excerpts">Excerpts</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Excerpts">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<ul><li><i>God the almighty granted more time to me than to my predecessors. This was granted to me not so much for me I know, as for the benefit of others</i>. (From the Letter of Chavara to his nephew Joseph Chavara.)<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><i> A good Christian family is the image of heaven, where persons are living together, by the bond of blood and affection, duly respecting and obeying their parents, and walking peacefully before the Lord and people, ensures their salvation, according to each one’s state of life</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><i>Just as without eyes one cannot see the material things of the world, so also without knowledge it will be impossible for us to see or understand the reality of this world and the eternity where God dwells in. As those who have no eyes are called “Blind”, so too those who have no learning are to be called “intellectually blind” Hence it is the responsibility of priest to teach the faithful and of parents to teach their children.</i><sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Images">Images</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Images">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara_1987_stamp_of_India.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara_1987_stamp_of_India.jpg/220px-Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara_1987_stamp_of_India.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="297" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara_1987_stamp_of_India.jpg/330px-Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara_1987_stamp_of_India.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/e/ec/Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara_1987_stamp_of_India.jpg 2x" data-file-width="349" data-file-height="471" /></a><figcaption>Kuriakose Elias Chavara on a 1987 stamp of India</figcaption></figure>
<p>There are various images of Kuriakose Elias Chavara all over the world. The <a href="/wiki/National_Shrine_of_Saint_Jude_(England)" title="National Shrine of Saint Jude (England)">National Shrine of Saint Jude</a>, Faversham, United Kingdom has a beautiful icon of the Chavara. In 2004 a fire broke out in the Shrine Chapel which destroyed the murals which hung there, and it damaged much of the other artwork. The decision was made to install icons depicting saints inspired by the Carmelite Rule of Saint Albert, and in commemoration of the 8th centenary of the Carmelite Rule in 2007. The icons were written by Sister Petra Clare, a Benedictine hermit living in Scotland, United Kingdom.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Museums">Museums</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Museums">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>There are many museums associated with life and activities of Kuriakose Elias Chavara. They significant ones are:
</p><p>St. Chavara Museum, Archives and Research Centre,  Mannanam.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Museum at Mannanam consists of the room he stayed, articles used by him, the documents written by him, the replica of the press he founded, the first school he started and above all it is located in the place where he founded the first monastery.
</p><p>St. Chavara Kuriakose Elias Museum, <a href="/wiki/Koonammavu" title="Koonammavu">koonammavu</a>
</p><p>This visitors of this museum can enter the room where Chavara died and can also see various articles used by him. This museum is associated with St. Philomena's Church Koonammavu.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Chavara National Museum, <a href="/wiki/Vazhakulam" title="Vazhakulam">Vazhakulam</a>
</p><p>The exhibits consists of a miniature model of Chavara's birth house, articles used by him and also various antique articles.<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Chavara Museum and Art Gallery, <a href="/wiki/Alappuzha" title="Alappuzha">Alappuzha</a>
</p><p>The exhibits in the museum consist of articles used by Chavara such as his cot, the chair used by him in the boat. One of the special exhibit is the box which was used to transfer the remains of Chavara from Koonammavu to Mannanam. It also has old holy Vestments, antique utilities and boxes. A number of books about Chavara are also displayed. Yet another attraction is the miniature model of his birth house.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<li id="cite_note-CMC-28"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-CMC_28-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"><cite class="citation web cs1"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.cmcsisters.org/home/inner/4">"Congregation of Mother of Carmel"</a>. cmcsisters.org.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=unknown&amp;rft.btitle=Congregation+of+Mother+of+Carmel&amp;rft.pub=cmcsisters.org&amp;rft_id=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.cmcsisters.org%2Fhome%2Finner%2F4&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3AKuriakose+Elias+Chavara" class="Z3988"></span></span>

<li id="cite_note-29"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-29">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><i>Diary of Fr. Leopold</i>, volume 3:25 (CMC Archives, Aluva). A notable problem posed by some is that the crucial phrase “<i>e fondato con somme fatiche il monastero delle Monache</i>” is written in between the lines, and it would have been added later by someone. However, it must also be noted that not only this one phrase, but four other such superscriptions are made, including a grammatical correction in the same text.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-30"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-30">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">In a book titled <i>The Founding Fathers of the TOCD in Kerala</i> (originally written in Malayalam, in 1871, soon after the death of Chavara, but published in 1905), the author Kuriakose Elias Porukkara, the second Prior of TOCD for men, deals with the long cherished dream of Chavara to found a convent for women and how he had set the scope of this new community of religious women: “Being there [Koonammavu], <i>[he] specially wished to make a convent of sisters as an abode of virtues for the girls of Malayalam, for learning doctrines and traditions of the Catholic religion and to grow as good Christian children</i>. Trusting in the Lord Almighty, accepted the persons who were prepared as its foundation and made a convent temporarily with bamboo mat for them to stay. In a miraculous way, within eight months, collecting Rs. 8000 as alms by different means, built the convent and arranged all the necessary things and made the sisters stay there and he felt happy seeing their modest life and virtues; allowed them to make their profession and finally established the Blessed Sacrament in their chapel. Just as Patriarch Jacob loved most his youngest son Benjamin, <i>he loved them most deeply, and took care of them as a mother takes care of her own children</i>” (page 35).</span>
</li>

</li>
